# spec-to-code map

> Maps spec clauses to the code coordinates that ground them, so the next
> session opens a coordinate instead of re-searching — locating the code is the
> expensive half, and this file exists so it is paid once. Written and checked
> by machines (`evidence-check`); committed so it follows worktrees and other
> machines.
>
> This is SpecSeal's ledger for SpecSeal, and it is the gathered half of one.
> **A work item writes `seal/ledger/<work-item-id>.md` instead of appending
> here** — two branches never queue at one file, because no two work items
> share an id. The checker reads both addresses. At each release,
> `.github/scripts/fold_ledger.py` moves every fragment into this file under a
> `## X.Y.Z — <date>` heading, one `###` section per work item marked with
> `<!-- specs/<work-item-id> -->` (the marker's text names the work item by its
> sub-directory and did not change when the root did), and removes the
> fragment. So this file grows once per release, by the rows that release's
> work items wrote, and the areas above those sections are the rows from
> before the fragments existed.

## Coordinates

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Coordinate notation | `path#anchor@hash` from the repo root |
| Trust exceptions | none |

A coordinate names **content, never a position**. The anchor is a symbol name
where the language has one — `.py` is read with Python's own parser — and a
distinctive line of text otherwise; the hash covers the region under it. A row
carries no line number and no commit SHA, so nothing in it goes stale for a
reason unrelated to the claim, and the check calls git for nothing — the one
exception is `--migrate`, which consults the old stamp's commit before it
trusts a line number it is rewriting.

The **Checked** column carries the date somebody read the code. Re-verifying a
row is re-reading it and running `evidence-check --reverify`, which recomputes
the hash and names what it changed.

**A row whose anchor a change removes is removed with it.** Its claim went
with the code; the new claim is a new row, in the work item's own fragment.
So a claim leaves this file when the code it was about does, and the fold at
the next release brings the new claim back in with the rest of its fragment.

## Scope decisions

Judgments that don't follow from code or documents alone.

| Decision | Content | Grounds |
|---|---|---|
| The ledger opens at one commit | Every row below is stamped at this tree's first commit rather than left to the header alone | A row with no stamp of its own measures from the header, and a header nobody re-reads re-dates every claim at once. Stamping them all at the baseline costs nothing today and makes the first genuine re-verification visible |

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The gate reads a heredoc body as commands, so a heredoc that only patches a file is judged by what its body says | `hooks/commit-review-gate.py#_hides_a_commit@2eab9355` | **Executed**, twice independently: a body carrying a commit on its own line returns True under both `cat > f <<'EOF'` and `python3 - <<'PY'`, and False for a body carrying none | 2026-08-31 | Deliberate, per legacy #75: a commit hidden in a body used to walk straight past. The agent files now route file edits through the `Edit` tool, so no command line reaches the reader at all. Whether the reader should skip a body being written to a file is Q1 of `specs/1788184145-the-gate-stops-the-session-editing-its-tests/questions.md` |
| Only a segment whose command word is `git` with the `commit` subcommand is counted, so a commit sitting inside a string literal is not | `hooks/commit-review-gate.py#_hides_a_commit@2eab9355`, assembled in `commit_invocations` at `hooks/commit-review-gate.py#commit_invocations@bd3f5b98` | **Executed**, round 1's probe. Whole fixture files handed to `_hides_a_commit` are clean — `tests/test_gate_judges_the_repo_it_commits_to.py` and `tests/test_what_the_reader_understands.py` both — while `agents/smith.md` and `README.md` TRIP, each only on its own `[no-review]` waiver example | 2026-08-31 | This refutes both readings that preceded it. It is NOT "the fixtures are shell command strings, so the reader finds a commit in them", and it is NOT "the documents are what trip it": issue #34's eight-line PARTIAL patch of a fixture trips as well. A fragment is judged on its own quoting, not on the file it came from |
| A segment the reader cannot expand counts as a commit, so a body with no `git` in it at all can stop the session | `hooks/commit-review-gate.py#_hides_a_commit@2eab9355`, resolved by `_eval_hides_a_commit` at `hooks/commit-review-gate.py#_eval_hides_a_commit@11406469`, against `EXPANDS` at `` hooks/cmdline.py#"EXPANDS = "$`*?[]{}""@6d56a043 `` | **Executed**, four bodies: `eval "$CMD"` TRIPS · `eval $(cat f)` TRIPS · `eval "echo hello"` clean (no expansion character) · `print('hello')` clean | 2026-08-31 | Fail-closed by design — the docstring says nothing can tell *reduces to a commit* from *reduces to something else* without running the shell. This is the branch a reader misses: searching a patch for a commit and finding none does not clear it. `tests/test_what_the_reader_understands.py#"("an eval", "eval 'cd %s'; git commit -m x", "eval 'cd %s'"),"@770c7def` is an `eval` fixture, so a partial patch of that file is exactly the case |
| Prose added to a document can flip the gate's verdict on that whole document without containing a commit at all | `hooks/commit-review-gate.py#_hides_a_commit@2eab9355` | **Executed** while fixing round 1. `agents/warden.md` is clean as a whole; adding one paragraph with a single apostrophe (`the fragment's own quoting`) made it TRIP, because the odd quote flipped the reader's state and put the `[no-review]` row in its probe table into command position. Removing the apostrophe restored `clean`. Round 2 confirmed the parity at the same coordinate: 0 → clean · 1 → TRIPS · 2 → clean · 3 → TRIPS | 2026-08-31 | **What is parity-sensitive is the apostrophes ADDED above the waiver row, not the file's absolute count** — measured after round 2's edit, `agents/warden.md` holds 11 apostrophes above that row, an odd number, and the file is still clean. Backticks and double quotes interact, so only the whole-file verdict answers this; a raw count does not. Measure above the waiver row, never below, because quote state flows forward. Pinned by `tests/test_edits_go_through_the_edit_tool.py::test_the_warden_file_does_not_itself_trip_the_commit_gate`. `agents/smith.md` is deliberately not pinned — it has tripped at its own waiver example since before this work |

## What the cost meter can read

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A turn is one assistant message carrying at least one tool_use, keyed by the message id with the row uuid and then the row itself as fallbacks — per-block counting pinned tools per turn at ~1.00 structurally | `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#load@d7f90cd4`, `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#strip_pipe@82b0813e` | **Executed**: six new cases in `tests/test_session_cost.py`, each shown red against the per-block code first — a two-call message read 1.0 where 2.0 is true, a split message was two turns, a keyless transcript is one turn per row (the old floor, never an inflated ratio), and a message's tokens counted once per call | 2026-09-06 | The phase commit that carried this is in the work item's `plan.md` Status column rather than here; a ledger row names no commit at all. Review round 1 established three more parser facts (executed there, read here): a result row never creates a turn even when its uuid differs from its call's; an unresolved tool_use joins the turn count but not the call count and drags the ratio down — 0.5 on a synthetic, negligible at a 65-call scale; non-monotonic timestamps split one turn into two gap groups and overstate model time, which an append-only transcript does not produce. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in work item 1788613827's round 1 fix pass**, which put the type check under the three-step fallback: an id that is not a string is skipped for the next candidate, where it used to go straight into a `set` and raise `TypeError: unhashable type`. The order of the three keys and the one-turn-per-row floor are unchanged; `message_key` is where they now live. **Re-read 2026-09-06 in the same work item's round 2 fix pass**, which guarded the one field in `load` whose readers had no guard of any kind — a `tool_use` block's `name`, normalised by `tool_name` before it is stored. Nothing about turn keying moved: the message id still outranks the row uuid, the row position is still the floor, and the three cases that pin that order are green. What the fix touches is the tuple `load` puts in `pending`, whose `name` slot two later readers consume |
| Model time runs from a turn's last result to the next turn's first call, so a batch's internal waits are the tools running, not the model thinking | `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#analyse@59d3d713` | **Executed**: `test_the_gap_after_a_batch_measures_from_its_last_result` books 7s where the per-call reading booked 15s, and the split-message case holds the intra-turn wait at zero model seconds | 2026-09-01 | The line-numbering caveat this row used to carry is gone with line numbers: the coordinate names a symbol and the hash covers its body, so there is no numbering for the tripwire to miss and nothing to re-stamp. The advisory wording forks at a ratio of exactly 1.0: above it, `most turns send a single call` replaces the one-at-a-time claim the number no longer supports. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in work item 1788613827's round 1 fix pass**, which added `call_turns` to what `analyse` returns — `tools_per_turn`'s own denominator, so the printed report can say which turns it counted. No gap, no turn boundary and no model-time arithmetic moved |

No conformance rule was added at either draft: a spawn prompt is not a file the protocol can check. The meter had sat unreferenced through a full day of measurements nobody took. Re-read 2026-09-04 against the enlarged section; the claim is unchanged and the note said `Draft 0.6` until that read. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in work item 1788597030's round 1 fix pass**, which added one paragraph to the section: the draft pull request precedes the handoff rather than travelling in it, opened when the build's last phase closes, with the review skill's section named as the owner. The four requirements and the pointer at the meter are unchanged. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in work item 1788613827's phase 4**, after phase 3 added two paragraphs to the `### After the run — the per-segment bars` subsection — the bars judge a segment and the run-level table judges a run, with a pointer at the table's owner. Nothing about the handoff's four requirements, the progress channel or the pointer at the meter moved. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in the same work item's round 1 fix pass**, which reworded one sentence of the same subsection — the run-level table's five glossed row names are gone, the pointer at the owner is where it was, and none of the four requirements is touched. **Re-read 2026-09-06 in work item 1788632199's phase 3**, after its phase 2 added a FIFTH requirement — a runner the repository ships is found, not typed into every prompt — and moved the count sentence with it, the way work item 1788501054 did for the fourth. The requirement the fifth narrows is the fourth, and that one survives intact for a repository shipping no runner. ### 1788229400-every-branch-appends-to-the-same-two-files

Rows for the work item that closed #46 and #52.

A coordinate here names content, never a position: a symbol name where the
language has one, a distinctive line of text otherwise, and a hash of the
region under it. No row carries a line number or a commit, so nothing in one
goes stale for a reason unrelated to the claim it makes.

#### A coordinate names content, not a position

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| An anchor resolves to a symbol span via the stdlib `ast`, or to a quoted line of text, and to nothing else | `skills/evidence-check/scripts/evidence_check.py#resolve_unit@c6839923`, `skills/evidence-check/scripts/evidence_check.py#py_spans@4045ba55` | **Executed**: `handler`, `Box` and `Box.open` each resolve to their own span, decorators included, and a file that will not parse yields `None`, which is the one case that falls through to the generic text rule — a file that parses and lacks the symbol is BROKEN | 2026-09-01 | Decorators are in the span because a decorator carries behaviour. No dependency is added — `ast` ships with Python |
| A change inside the anchored region drifts; an edit that only moves the region does not | `skills/evidence-check/scripts/evidence_check.py#check_text@2328e19a`, `skills/evidence-check/scripts/evidence_check.py#content_hash@5c2ba0ae` | **Executed against this repository's own ledger**, not only a fixture: inserting a statement into `_hides_a_commit` reports `1 drifted`, and inserting a line above everything in the same file leaves `34 ok` | 2026-09-01 | The second edit moved every line number in the file. Under the line-number scheme it was the case that read OK while pointing at the wrong lines |
| Trailing whitespace and blank lines are normalised away; indentation is not | `skills/evidence-check/scripts/evidence_check.py#normalise@e7d71707` | **Executed** at the unit and by mutation: normalising indentation too leaves a dedent silent, and that mutation turns exactly one case red | 2026-09-01 | In Python a dedent moves a statement out of the block it belonged to. A hash that shrugged at it would go quiet where the edit matters most |

The catch is still `Exception` and the mark is still written before the group decides, so the claim holds. **That stamp names no commit any more** — #239 moved every rider to `Verified <date> against <anchor>@<hash>`, which is why this row's hash moved again without its claim moving; the sentence above is what the 2026-09-05 re-read found, kept because it is the incident #239 removes |

|---|---|---|---|---|

### 1788360817-the-pull-request-language-is-fixed-inside-a-skill

<!-- This work item's ledger rows: one file per work item so that two branches
never queue at seal/ledger.md, folded into it at the release.

Every row here is over PROSE, and that is the work item rather than a gap in
it. Nothing in `hooks/` reads `seal/config.md`: judging what language a
commit message is in means being wrong about names, identifiers and quoted
English, and a wrong stop blocks a correct commit (`questions.md` Q3). So
each row's code grounds are the document that carries the rule and the case
that pins it, and "Executed" means the case was run, not that a session was
observed obeying the rule. The one thing no case here can reach is named in
`overview.md` §Not verified. -->

###### The row, and where a session reads it from

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 · the skill names both the file and the row, so a session knows where to look and what to look for | `skills/commit-pr-convention/SKILL.md#"## The language is the repository's, and it says so in a file"@a76da269`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_the_skill_names_the_file_and_the_row@cbca742e` | **Executed**: 24 passed in the new file at `56ec644`. Both halves are asserted separately — a path with no row name and a row name with no path each leave a session unable to act | 2026-09-03 | The row is the only thing in `config.md` today; the table is open for more items later |
| S2 / 🟡 3 · every way of NOT naming a language lands on English, and there are FOUR: no file, no such row, an empty value, and a file that cannot be read or does not parse as that table | `skills/commit-pr-convention/SKILL.md#"## The language is the repository's, and it says so in a file"@a76da269`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#configured_language@4e389a05`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_every_way_of_not_naming_a_language_lands_on_english@b00fbf02`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_an_unreadable_config_lands_on_english_too@ea7e7742`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_the_skill_names_every_way_of_not_naming_one@73ae726b`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_the_default_is_stated_where_the_path_is@8fc71b49` | **Executed**: the rule is now RUN, not only asserted in prose — a reader built to the skill's sentence answers `English` for every shape, the unreadable one built as a `config.md` that is a directory (`IsADirectoryError`, an `OSError`, the class chosen so a permission error lands the same way). 46 passed in the file | 2026-09-03 | Round 1 🟡 3: the first draft named two absences and stopped, so a session meeting an empty value could stop, ask, or guess. Round 2 ❓ 8 settled the COUNT at four — "cannot be read or does not parse" is one way with two spellings — and `UNNAMED` keeps five ids for it, which its comment now says. The old row cited `test_absence_means_english_in_both_of_its_spellings`, which round 1 split |
| S3 · the root is resolved `<repo>/seal/` then `<git-common-dir>/seal/`, never spelled as the first alone | `skills/commit-pr-convention/SKILL.md#"## The language is the repository's, and it says so in a file"@a76da269`, `tests/test_the_pull_request_language_is_the_repositorys.py#test_the_root_is_resolved_rather_than_spelled@6181a8fe` | **Executed**: both `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` and `<repo>/seal/` are asserted present, so neither place can be dropped alone. **Read**: `hooks/optin.py#home_at` is the code that does this for the hooks; no hook reads `config.md`, so the skill's sentence is the whole mechanism here | 2026-09-03 | Spelling `<repo>/seal/` and stopping is what left a local-mode ledger unread at every commit in #80 — the same mistake, one release earlier |

###### The mirror, and the file itself

### 1788398967-local-modes-records-never-leave-the-clone

<!-- This work item's ledger rows: one file per work item so that two branches
never queue at seal/ledger.md, folded into it at the release.

Every "Executed" here names a run whose output was read. Two rows say
"Executed" about a claim that was FALSE when it was written and is corrected
in place — the `extractall` pair — because the measurement is the row's whole
value: a later session reading only the corrected sentence would not know the
claim had ever been the other way, and would be tempted to widen it back. -->

<!-- Four rows OUTSIDE this fragment drifted on this branch and were
re-verified rather than re-pointed, because every one of their claims still
holds. Three cite `hooks/optin.py#home_at` — two in `seal/ledger.md` and one
in #80's fragment — which this work rewrote without changing what it answers.
Two cite the whole-document anchor on `templates/seal-README.md`, which this
work added a paragraph to.

The second group is #80's own 🟡 4 — *a whole-document anchor makes any edit
to that document another work item's drift, which a fragment-scoped check
cannot see* — and it has now demonstrated itself. Nothing here acts on it; it
is recorded so the next reader knows that row describes something observed
twice rather than once. -->

#### The root's two places

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|

Hoisting it is what let the two places be produced in one place |

<!-- specs/1788411058-the-mode-is-two-shell-lines-in-a-readme -->
### 1788411058-the-mode-is-two-shell-lines-in-a-readme

<!-- This work item's ledger rows: one file per work item so that two branches
never queue at seal/ledger.md, folded into it at the release.

The rows split in two. The first table is about a rule that is PROSE — the
row is declared state and nothing at runtime may read it — so its code
grounds are the module that does the resolving and the case that pins the
absence. The rest are behaviour, and "Executed" there means the case was run
against the shipped code.

Two rows rest on measurements rather than on reading: what `git rm -r
--cached` does to a staged edit, and what each of the two shipped checkers
does in a repository with no `seal/`. Both were run on 2026-09-03 and both
changed what the code does. -->

###### The row is declared state, and the folder still decides

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-row · `seal/config.md`'s `Mode` row says where the root SHOULD be; `hooks/optin.py#home_at` says where it IS, and stays the only reader of that question | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#declared@1c170f62`, `hooks/optin.py#home_at@702af479`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_the_report_names_the_folder_the_row_and_whether_they_agree@997faec8` | **Executed**: the report names both and the cases assert what the FOLDER holds after every switch, never the row's word for it. **Read**: that no hook opens `config.md` — `grep` over `hooks/` finds no reader, and this row is what a later one would have to overturn | 2026-09-03 | A gate that trusted the row would open a place with no folder, and `optin` is documented to fail toward "not opted in" rather than toward a guess |
| S2 · the row has NO default. An absent one is written from where the folder is, which is the state of every repository that has a `config.md` today | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#mode_report@94f925b8`, `templates/config.md#"## Mode"@325a82df`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_an_absent_row_is_written_from_where_the_folder_is@113b9e72` | **Executed**: eight cases, four absent shapes × both modes. Running it in one mode only would pass for a fixed default of `shared`; the mutation that writes `shared` regardless reddens four of them | 2026-09-03 | The first-setup question's default (shared, offered first) is a different thing: it applies where the folder exists at NEITHER place, and it is an answer a person gives rather than a value code falls back to |
| S5 · a value that is neither mode is a claim nobody can act on — reported, fails `--check`, refuses `--apply`, and never overwritten | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#declared@1c170f62`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_a_value_that_is_not_a_mode_is_a_claim_nobody_can_act_on@570b5fff` | **Executed**: the report leaves the row as it found it, which is the half that matters — treating it as "absent" would write over what a person meant | 2026-09-03 | The four ways of NOT declaring (absent, empty, unparseable, no file) all pass; this fifth shape is not one of them |

###### What the switch touches, as a class

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S15 · the guard refuses what the INDEX can lose and names an untracked file as travelling. `git rm -r --cached` drops a staged edit out of the index and prints nothing | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#indexed@1ff97a21`, `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#porcelain@a9cc6d66`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_a_change_the_index_carries_refuses@7343ce5d`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_an_untracked_file_travels_with_the_folder@beeacc70` | **Executed** 2026-09-03, four tree states against `git rm -r --cached seal`: clean, a staged modification, a worktree modification, and an untracked extra. All four exit 0 and leave every file on disk; the staged one loses its index content silently. That measurement is the guard's whole grounds, and it is also why untracked does not refuse | 2026-09-03 | Refusing over an untracked file made the ordinary first run refuse: the bare command writes an absent row, and the switch met the file it had just written |

| S18 · the guard watches the workflow path as well as the root, because the switch stages both | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#refusals@495488d6`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_a_dirty_workflow_path_refuses@f15f66ff` | **Executed**: the mutation that watches only the root reddens exactly this case | 2026-09-03 | #81 closed "a path this command writes through" three times, one name apart each time. The five paths are enumerated in `spec.md` §"What the switch touches" rather than fixed where a finding points |
| S16 / S17 · `lexists` at the destination and `islink` at the source, so a link is never moved and never written through | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#refusals@495488d6`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_a_destination_that_already_exists_refuses@62a9068e`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_a_source_that_is_a_symbolic_link_refuses@6d81a616` | **Executed** on macOS, three destination shapes including a broken link, which `exists` reads as absent. **Not run** on Windows, where the link cases skip for want of the privilege | 2026-09-03 | The same pair `write_zip` and `place` were fixed for, one command over |

###### The workflow file, which is the half a `mv` leaves behind

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S21 · a workflow left behind after a switch to local is not untidiness. The two shipped checkers fail in OPPOSITE directions in a repository with no `seal/` | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#remove_workflow@dcb7ea33`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_shared_to_local_removes_the_workflow_this_plugin_wrote@707ee74e` | **Executed** 2026-09-03 in a repository with no root: `unverified_check.py --baseline <ref> seal/specs/` exits **2** ("no such path"), `chain_check.py --baseline <ref>` exits **0** having examined nothing. So the leftover is a build red forever beside a check reporting a pass it never earned | 2026-09-03 | `templates/hygiene.yml`'s header already stated both exit codes correctly; nothing acted on them. A handoff to this work item asserted both pass, and the measurement is what corrected it |
| S22 / S23 / S25 · the file is removed only when it is this plugin's, written only when the path is free, and not written at all when the version cannot be read | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#install_workflow@b16c89ba`, `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#remove_workflow@dcb7ea33`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_a_workflow_this_plugin_did_not_write_is_left_alone@e1b458a8`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_a_version_that_cannot_be_read_writes_no_workflow@b5144173` | **Executed**: the marker is the plugin's clone URL, and a case pins that `templates/hygiene.yml` still carries it, so the two cannot drift apart. The version case removes `plugin_version` alone — removing the whole plugin root took the template with it and passed through the wrong branch | 2026-09-03 | A workflow written with `v<version>` still in it fails CI's `git clone --branch` at the first pull request, which is worse than no workflow |

###### What a person is told

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S12 · local → shared is said to be the door before it acts, and the door is the COMMIT rather than the move | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#switch@0b1b6da7`, `docs/one-root-by-lifetime.md#"### Shared or local"@a47b3694`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_going_to_shared_says_the_commit_is_the_door@e5ff9e66` | **Executed**: the line is asserted to precede the instruction to commit, and to name the way back. The command stages and never commits, which is what keeps that moment the person's | 2026-09-03 | The direction local mode exists to prevent is the one that cannot be walked back. `questions.md` Q3 records that this is a line rather than a flag, and what would overturn it |
| S26 · a clone with more than one worktree is named rather than refused | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#other_worktrees@ddf91b71`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_another_worktree_is_named@ce0815d1` | **Executed** 2026-09-03: switching shared → local from a linked worktree leaves the main worktree holding the committed root on its own branch, so the two read different roots until the commit reaches both. Nothing is lost — the other copy is the committed one — and it heals at checkout | 2026-09-03 | Refusals here are for states where something would be lost or half-moved. `questions.md` Q7 has the alternative |
| S12 · every string this command prints for a person to paste works from any directory, and under the failure its own message diagnoses | `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#switch@0b1b6da7`, `skills/implement/scripts/seal.py#install_workflow@b16c89ba`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_the_recovery_commands_work_where_they_are_printed@a75063a7`, `tests/test_the_mode_is_a_row_and_a_command.py#test_the_way_back_it_names_works_from_a_subdirectory@ec9fd6fd` | **Executed** 2026-09-03 on git 2.50.1: all eight printed commands enumerated and run from a subdirectory. Before the fixes, five exited 128 with `did not match any files` or exited 0 having done nothing; one failed at the repository root too, under the ignore rule its own message diagnoses, for want of `-f`. `:/` resolves to the top of the worktree it is run in — inside a linked worktree it means that worktree's top and leaves the main one's index alone | 2026-09-03 | Four rounds fixed one member of this at a time: two sentences, then three commands. The enumeration is what closed it |

### 1788433011-every-spawn-prompt-is-retyped-from-memory

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Five rows, and the last of them is a defect this work item produced and its
own last phase found. Recorded rather than repaired quietly, because the
mechanism that let it through is the general lesson and the fix is one line. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 · `skills/agent-contract/SKILL.md` is the single home for every rule true of all agents, and a definition that carries a section's own sentences fails rather than passing unnoticed | `tests/test_a_moved_rule_leaves_its_definition.py#test_no_definition_carries_a_sections_own_sentences@546722ba`, `skills/agent-contract/SKILL.md#"## How the sections are numbered"@838b3c7d` | **Executed**: the case is parametrised on both globs, so it is one case per section per definition and a §17 or a fourth agent is checked on the day it lands. Its mutation — a section's body pasted back under a new heading — is red for every pair | 2026-09-03 | The half of Q2 that says *the definition keeps only its own application* had nothing behind it until this module. Measured by the orchestrator at `e51fd0b`: §9 pasted into `agents/warden.md` left `test_broad_gate_rule`, `test_edits_go_through_the_edit_tool`, `test_every_agent_reads_the_contract` and `test_the_agent_contract_holds_the_universal_rules` all green, 81 passed, because every assertion in them is a presence check |
| L2 · the duplication check is a 15-word verbatim window, and both ends of its margin are measured rather than chosen | `tests/test_a_moved_rule_leaves_its_definition.py#test_the_window_sits_between_what_was_measured@412468cc` | **Executed**: the longest run any KEPT application shares with any section is **10** words — `agents/smith.md`'s *a bare word is a pathspec and git rejects it*, which §8 and the design gate's waiver paragraph both spell because both are about one git fact. The smallest run a REAL duplication produced, measured on the same files before phase 4 removed them, is **25** (§10), then 31 (§9), 37 (§16), 59 (§11) and 66 (§3) | 2026-09-03 | This is the number a later session would otherwise re-derive, and re-deriving it needs the pre-phase-4 files. The case prints both bounds and fails when either end of the margin closes, so raising the window to quiet a failure is red rather than silently mis-tuned. Verbatim is deliberate: a paraphrase check would have to decide when two sentences say the same thing, and it would refuse the application prose the rule exists to permit |
| L3 · the protocol is a pointer, and the sentence a prompt is actually governed by is what stands where the rules stood | `docs/review-handoff-protocol.md#"## What every spawn prompt used to carry / ### What a prompt is left holding"@2de7af10`, `docs/review-handoff-protocol.md#"## What every spawn prompt used to carry / ### Where each half went"@4fe0b4fb`, `tests/test_the_handoff_before_round_one.py#test_the_protocol_no_longer_states_the_rules_it_points_at@7f94150d` | **Executed**: the presence case asserts the section names the contract path, names `agents/<name>.md`, and still says *a prompt carries what is specific to the round and nothing else*; the absence case asserts five phrases verbatim from what phase 5 removed, so a paste-back is red rather than invisible | 2026-09-03 | The section title moved with the content — *What every spawn prompt carries, until the definitions carry it* asserted an interim state that stopped being true in the same commit. A heading is what a reader scanning the document acts on, and one document named the old title |
| L4 · the orchestrator is bound by the same contract, and the two skills it reads are where that is written | `skills/code-review/SKILL.md#"## Orchestrator: a fix pass resumes the implementer"@c0dc63a1`, `skills/implement/SKILL.md#"load it.** A session driving the work directly reads this skill and never"@21ad749e` | **Read**, both paragraphs, this session. Nothing at runtime reads either — the binding is prose that a session obeys — so what can be checked is that the two files say it and that neither restates a rule the contract holds. **Executed** 2026-09-03, re-measuring what phase 5 recorded: each added paragraph's longest run against any section is **4** words, not the 8 that work item's memo claimed, and both are far under L2's 15-word window | 2026-09-03 | `agents/*.md` cannot reach this party, and the headline failure was an orchestrator breaking a rule it had put into every prompt it sent. `user-invocable: false` permits the load and does not oblige it, so the obligation has to be written where the orchestrator reads. The `implement` coordinate is a line anchor rather than a heading path because the paragraph sits above the first `##` and the enclosing heading is the whole 741-line file — a hash over that would drift on every unrelated edit, which is the failure a coordinate exists to avoid. **What the re-measurement also found, and this work item did not decide:** measured over the WHOLE of `skills/implement/SKILL.md` rather than the added paragraph, the file shares a 30-word sentence with §5 — *is not a coordinate: the number can be checked while the claim it stands for cannot, and one such fact reached five documents before a review round found it false*. That prose predates the contract and Q2's answer scoped the single-home rule to `agents/*.md`, so nothing here is red; it is recorded because the next reader measuring the file rather than the paragraph will find the same 30 and have no way to tell it was seen. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in work item 1788597030's round 1 fix pass**, which added the fix-table paragraph to the review skill's section; the sentence binding the orchestrator to the contract is untouched, and the `implement` line anchor did not move |
| L5 · a rule with one written home is deleted by the move that was built to stop rules going missing, and an absence check alone cannot see it | `agents/smith.md#"## Phases"@05d0fa22`, `tests/test_the_handoff_before_round_one.py#test_the_smiths_definition_mandates_mutating_every_unit_it_added@6a971c8f` | **Executed** 2026-09-03: at `dcdf4e4` a search for *mutation-test every unit added* across `agents/`, `skills/`, `docs/`, `templates/`, `CLAUDE.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.md` returned nothing. The new presence case is shown red four ways — the rule deleted, the cadence changed, the timing dropped, the observation dropped — and the first draft's `one at a time` assertion PASSED under the second, because the design gate holds that phrase in an unrelated sentence a hundred lines up | 2026-09-04 | The row above it in the same module asserts the phrase has LEFT the protocol, and it went green over a rule that had left the repository altogether. That is the general claim: an absence check with no presence check beside it cannot tell a rule that moved from a rule that was deleted. The other four rows of that table point at contract sections, which `test_the_agent_contract_holds_the_universal_rules.py` pins from the other side; this one pointed at a definition and nothing pinned it. Contract §15 is the neighbour and not the rule — it asks that one case be seen red on the day it is planted, where this asks that every unit a branch added be broken before the branch leaves. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in work item 1788597030's round 1 fix pass**: the region gained the fix-pass paragraph at step 3 — the `## Fixes` table, one row per OPEN finding, a correction closing `answered — corrected at <sha>` — the reach-back sentence naming `round_record.py new`, and the depth measured by `close` rather than declared. The mutation rule at step 4 is untouched. **Re-read 2026-09-06 in work item 1788632199's phase 3**: step 4 gained a paragraph telling a segment to find the runner its repository ships before inventing a command, and to type the narrow form. It sits above the mutation rule and touches neither it nor the moving-rule claim this row is about. **Re-read 2026-09-08 in work item 1788826000's round 1 fix pass**: the only edit to `## Phases` was the design-gate rider's own stamp, re-anchored from the sentence it quoted to this heading, so the region moved and the claim did not. Step 4's mutation rule is untouched |
| L6 · the record-language instruction has two homes and neither is a definition — the whole list in `implement`, the short form as contract §11 | `skills/agent-contract/SKILL.md#"## §11 The language the records are written in"@bdd6d99f`, `skills/implement/SKILL.md#"## The language the records are written in"@f307fad8` | **Executed**: the suite over the modules that read the definitions, 604 passed at `5e610a3` | 2026-09-03 | Replaces row S2 of work item 1788420760, removed from `seal/ledger.md` by this branch. That row's grounds named `agents/smith.md` and `agents/warden.md`, and phases 3 and 4 moved the section out of both — the coordinates read BROKEN, which is CI's failing exit rather than its warning |
| L7 · the probe rules and the git-from-Python rule are contract §7 and §8, and the definitions point rather than restate | `` `skills/agent-contract/SKILL.md#"## §7 A probe is named `test_tmp_*`, one file, run once, deleted"@9ac6eed4` ``, `tests/test_a_probe_that_commits_says_so.py#test_the_contract_carries_all_three_shapes@287934dc`, `tests/test_a_probe_that_commits_says_so.py#test_the_fact_finder_points_at_the_rule_it_no_longer_restates@9a67764a` | **Executed**: the module's cases now read the contract for the shapes and the definition for the pointer, which is the re-pointing Q2 answered `full` to | 2026-09-03 | Replaces the row under *An agent can write a probe that commits*, which claimed BOTH agent files carry the three shapes. `spec.md` records why that claim was always half wrong: the smith writes probes too and nothing said so anywhere, so the rule reached two agents of three |
| L8 · the contract-override refusal stands ONCE, as §3, with the 28-minute case named in it | `skills/agent-contract/SKILL.md#"## §3 A spawn prompt cannot widen the verification scope"@207e4d1d`, `tests/test_broad_gate_rule.py#test_the_refusal_is_stated_once_and_names_both_directions@174490ef`, `tests/test_broad_gate_rule.py#test_the_prohibition_itself_has_one_home_and_it_is_the_contract@321739a9` | **Executed**: both cases green at `5e610a3`, and the second is the one that would go red if a definition took a copy back | 2026-09-03 | Replaces the row claiming the refusal *stands in both agent contracts*, whose five smith coordinates and one warden coordinate all drifted when phases 3 and 4 removed the prose. ### 1788445862-a-phase-hands-the-next-one-a-record

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Five rows. This work item is templates, agent instructions, skill wiring and
tests — no hook, no gate, no checker — so most of what it built is already
pinned by the two new test modules and needs no coordinate of its own. What
earns a row here is what a future session could not cheaply re-derive from
those tests alone: a naming split that reads as an inconsistency until you
open the reasoning behind it (P3), and a testing pitfall this branch caught
in itself before it shipped (P5). -->

The real-records case is the other half — nothing had ever run these checks against `seal/specs/*/rounds/*.md`, so the first reader of a record this repository writes was the pull request it was written for. Re-read 2026-09-04 by work item 1788501054, which changed `fix_surface` and drifted this anchor: the new arm sits inside the `says_none` branch and reads no separator, so the ordering of the three limits is unchanged. **Re-read again 2026-09-04 in that item's round 3 fix pass**, which drifted this anchor a second time: nothing executable moved — the docstring gained a paragraph about the arm's key, and the `value.split(";")` that runs before the arrow and the comma is still the first thing either row meets. |

| F1 · the ladder's guard asks whether a failed create landed by looking for an open issue that is **not** the one `main` just closed, and it gives an empty reading the same one retry every other empty reading in this module gets | `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#landed_create@9deecaf4`, `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#open_issue@fcf5412c`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_the_issue_main_just_closed_does_not_count_as_the_create_landing@d89d0be1`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_the_landed_create_guard_retries_an_empty_reading@5e81444a` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at round 1's fix pass: 18 cases in that module, exit 0. Both cases were seen red at `b1f7340` before the guard existed, and four mutations killed — the exclusion removed, the exclusion inverted, the retry removed, and the sleep removed | 2026-09-06 | Round 1's 🔴 1 and 🟡 2. The first version of this guard read the raw open-issue list, and `main` closes the current log seconds before the ladder runs — so a `gh issue list` lagging that close answers with the closed issue, the guard reads it as the create landing, and the release ends with no rolling log open and the workflow green. This module's docstring is what the guard leaned on, and it says a lag can only ever produce a reading short a result. That was written about a reading taken after a label **add**. A reading taken after a **close** is the case it does not cover, and it produces exactly the extra issue the sentence rules out. The path is not exceptional either: whenever the milestone cannot be resolved, which is the case the ladder exists for, the first rung always fails and the guard always runs. The retry's sleep is the half a later reader drops as noise — an immediate second read hits the same lagged index, and removing it left every case here green until the case pinned it. **Re-read 2026-09-06 in work item 1788661274's phase 3**, which drifted the anchor without touching the guard: that branch's phase 1 renamed `open_issue`'s version parameter and moved the title behind `log_title`, and `landed_create` — where the exclusion, the retry and the sleep all live — did not drift at all. The hash is re-stamped by hand rather than by a blanket `--reverify --ledger seal/ledger.md`, because that run cannot narrow below a file and would also re-stamp `templates/config.md`'s pre-existing drift, which no session on that branch read |

What that takes away is the only path by which a create failing all the way down still names the already-closed issue and the title to open by hand — after which the release ends with zero open issues, silently, and the next one fails on a state nobody was told about. `try_run` exists for calls whose failure must not stop a release, and the create is not one of them: only the two arguments hanging off it are. **Re-read 2026-09-06 in work item 1788661274's phase 3.** The ladder is untouched — the last rung still calls `run` and the two above it still call `try_run`, and `try_run` did not drift. What moved is the title the rungs write, which drifted `open_issue` and the expected title inside the case. Re-stamped by hand, for the reason F1 states |

<!-- specs/1788491830-a-segments-record-says-what-it-cost -->
### 1788491830-a-segments-record-says-what-it-cost

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Four rows, and each is a narrow reading a later tidy-up would widen in good
faith: a branch order that looks arbitrary (R1), a regex whose whitespace
looks like slack (R2), two needles in one parametrised list that look
inconsistent with each other (R3), and two templates saying one rule at two
lengths where the shorter one reads better and is wrong (R4).

R2 and R3 came out of the mutation loop rather than out of reading the code,
and both were SURVIVORS — the case existed, looked like it pinned the thing,
and stayed green against the mutation of the thing it named. That is the class
this work item produced twice, and the rows are here so the next reader meets
it before the loop does. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 · `runner_problem` tries the `unknown` branch **before** the `on` split, and that order settles what a cell is read AS and never whether it is accepted | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#runner_problem@44124ee0`, `tests/test_a_record_says_what_ran_it.py#test_the_arm_order_changes_the_reading_and_never_the_verdict@e8b243f6` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `6bd730c`: the cited case now BUILDS the other arm order from the module's constants and compares the two on the verdict across 756 constructed cells, zero disagreements. Verified three ways — green unmutated, green with the real arms reversed (which is the claim, not a hole in it), and red naming the cell when an arm's separator set is changed so the orders come apart | 2026-09-04 | Round 1 wrote this row after disproving the load-bearing claim, and cited a case that did not run it — round 2's 🟡 6. That case asserted three values each asserted verbatim elsewhere plus `ON_RE.search("unknown") is None`, which cannot fail, since `unknown` holds no `on` substring at all. Reversing the real arms left it green. **A ledger row pointing at a case that does not execute its claim is the mistake this row's own Notes named one sentence later**, which is why the row is worth keeping rather than deleting: the claim is right, the grounds were a story, and the gap between those two is what a reader has to be able to see. What the order buys is the READING a message quotes back — `unknown — the model was not recorded on this run` is an unknown with a reason here and a pair whose model is `this run` under the other order, and both pass. A later reader may reverse it without breaking a verdict; what they would break is the sentence the refusal prints |
| R2 · `ON_RE` requires whitespace on **both** sides, and the word that pins it has to split into two NON-empty halves — `monitor`, not `carbon` | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#ON_RE@17c6f7c5`, `tests/test_a_record_says_what_ran_it.py#test_the_on_must_stand_alone@a76f5757` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `2a36737`: the pattern mutated to a bare `on` and the case exits 1 on `monitor`. Against `carbon` alone the same mutation left every case green, which is how the gap was found | 2026-09-04 | The refusal has two clauses in sequence — *not two halves* and *a half is empty* — and `carbon` trips the second, so a case carrying only that word is satisfied by a clause other than the one it names. Stripping the `\s+` from the pattern is the obvious simplification (the row already says `on`, and the whitespace reads as slack), and it silently makes the `on` inside a name a separator. The general form is worth more than the instance: **a case whose input, needle or fixture is satisfied by something other than the thing it means to pin is green against its own mutation.** This work item has now produced six, and the shape moved each time — an input tripping an earlier clause (this row), a needle satisfied by prose around the row (R3), an exit code satisfied by an unreachable commit (round 1's 🟡 5), a fixture with one record where the claim needed two (round 2's 🟡 7), an assertion that cannot fail at all (round 2's 🟡 6), and a needle satisfied by the same phrase in a neighbouring subsection (found in the mutation loop for round 2's own fix, three times in one run). None was found by reading; every one was found by mutating |
| R3 · `INSTRUCTORS` pins the three row-carrying files on the ROW and the two skills on the NAME, and the asymmetry is deliberate | `tests/test_a_record_says_what_ran_it.py#INSTRUCTORS@addb97b1`, `tests/test_a_record_says_what_ran_it.py#test_every_instructing_file_names_the_row@a2fe73e9` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `bf2eddc`: `docs/review-handoff-protocol.md`'s `| Ran by |` renamed to `| Run by |` and the case exits 1. Against the bare-name needle the same mutation left it green | 2026-09-04 | Making the five needles uniform is the tidy-up a reader reaches for, and it is what the case looked like before the mutation caught it. `docs/review-handoff-protocol.md` names `Ran by` in its own `####` section and in the drafts log as well as in the table, so the bare name survives the row being renamed out of existence — the document still says the word while the row a session copies is gone. The two skills carry no table at all, so the row spelling is not available to pin there, which is why the split is asymmetric rather than an oversight |
| R4 · `templates/sdd-phase.md` separates whose the value is from whose keystrokes fill it, and the round template's sentence copied across unchanged makes the row unfillable by anyone present | `` `templates/sdd-phase.md#"# <work-item-id> — phase <N>"@9146ea0e` ``, `tests/test_a_phase_hands_the_next_one_a_record.py#test_the_template_resolves_who_types_a_phase_records_row@460d1dbc`, `tests/test_a_record_says_what_ran_it.py#test_verify_says_how_a_phase_record_comes_to_carry_the_row@ebdd6741` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `473765d`: three mutations killed — the spawn-prompt half removed, the transcribe/decide distinction removed, and the sentence naming who writes a phase record removed | 2026-09-04 | The rule is one act for `rounds/round-N.md`, because the orchestrator knows what ran the round and runs `round_record.py new`, which writes that file from the `--ran-by` value it is handed. A phase record is written by the segment that just ran, so *the spawning session fills it, never the segment itself* — true of the round record and copied here verbatim in the first draft — tells the only party holding the pen not to write the row. The correction is not a softening: the value is still the orchestrator's to SOURCE, and what stays refused is the segment sourcing it from its own idea of what it is. A later editor tightening the two templates back into one sentence for symmetry re-opens exactly this, and the shorter sentence is the one that reads better. **Re-read 2026-09-05 in work item 1788597030's round 1 fix pass**, which moved the template's one sentence about the round record: the orchestrator runs `round_record.py new`, which writes that file from `--ran-by`. The distinction this row is about — whose the value is against whose keystrokes fill it — is untouched, and the sentence at the head of these Notes is corrected the same way (round 1's ⬜ 9 of that item) |
| R5 · a phase record's field table carries `Phase`, `Commit` and `Ran by` — three rows, not two — and its three sections still ship outside HTML comments as placeholders rather than filled claims | `templates/sdd-phase.md#"\| Field \| Value \|"@d59fb237`, `tests/test_a_phase_hands_the_next_one_a_record.py#test_the_field_table_is_phase_commit_and_what_ran_it@67075610`, `tests/test_a_phase_hands_the_next_one_a_record.py#test_all_three_sections_exist_outside_comments@f2f6b8d9` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 at `a8d899d`: the case asserts the exact list and goes red on any other, and the sections case is unchanged and green | 2026-09-04 | **Replaces P1 in `seal/ledger.md`, which this branch removed rather than re-pointed.** P1 claimed the table carries ONLY `Phase`/`Commit`, and adding `Ran by` made that false rather than stale — the claim went with the code, which is the case `CLAUDE.md` says is a removal and not a re-anchor. Its second anchor was BROKEN as well, because the case it named was renamed to say what the table now holds. The pin is an exact-list equality rather than a membership test, so it goes red whichever direction the template and the list move apart — worth knowing before a fourth row is added, because the equivalent round-record pin is a membership test and only goes red in one |

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |

| R1 · `added_on_branch` reads the LATEST add, and `--diff-filter=A` is what keeps *added* from collapsing into *touched* — the two together, because each is undefended by the cases the other one needs | `skills/code-review/scripts/chain_check.py#added_on_branch@5461cc08`, `tests/test_a_record_precedes_the_fixes_it_commissions.py#test_a_record_added_before_the_base_and_updated_on_the_branch_passes@53889300`, `tests/test_a_record_precedes_the_fixes_it_commissions.py#test_a_record_deleted_and_re_added_after_the_fix_is_judged_on_the_later_add@4dee6e90` | **Executed** 2026-09-04 after round 1: `found[0]` → `found[-1]` turns the delete-and-re-add case red and nothing else; dropping `--diff-filter=A` turns TWO cases red, the base-moved one and the updated-in-place one. The earliest add is the permissive direction: a stub committed on time, deleted, and the real record written after the fixes passes on the strength of a commit that no longer holds any of its content. Note what changed when the index flipped: under `found[-1]` dropping the flag was separable only by a base that MOVES under a long branch, and under `found[0]` it also refuses every correctly updated record, because the newest commit that TOUCHED a record is its verdict update. **A row written for the old index would now be false**, which is why this row names the pair rather than the flag alone |

Without the filter the refusal is not a rare false positive, it is red on the second record of every well-run chain — including this repository's own |

The inode fold answers all three plus symlinks with one rule. **Two ways out of the fold, and round 2's 🟡 8 is that only one of them existed**: `os.stat` raising reaches the fallback, and a `st_ino` of 0 did not — Python's contract makes a zero no identity at all, and taken at face value it gave every unstattable file ONE identity, so a ledger that was read swallowed every ledger that was not. Executed on a case-insensitive macOS volume: one file, one inode, TWO fallback identities. So the fallback OVER-reports off Windows, which is the declared direction, and the same holds for the two spellings the platform keeps apart anywhere. The paragraph declaring `normcase` unreachable was false too: it tied reachability to the zeroed inode, and `OSError` reaches the fallback on every platform — what is Windows-only is `normcase` differing from the identity, so it is unkillable off Windows and load-bearing on it. Nothing goes unread either way, and the conclusion is unchanged; only both reasons moved |

Mutation 2 guarded only the shapes the finding named and was killed by the `null` case. **The method is the durable half.** Round 1 closed this class by reading the file, said six members, and left the seventh three lines below one of the six — the enumeration is what turns *I looked and found no more* into a number a later session can re-run and compare. `bool`, `int` and `float` names are not members: they hash and they format, so they print a number where a tool name goes, which is the wrong-value direction `count`'s docstring refuses to take for tokens and accepts here, since a name is not summed. **Work item `1788700685` found this row's guarantee stated one axis wider than its own enumeration, and the row is corrected in place rather than removed.** Removal is for a row whose anchor a change takes out of the tree; every anchor here still resolves, the four funnels still type-check, and phase 1 ADDED to two of them. What was over-stated is the reach: the enumeration crosses fields with JSON types, so a value of the type the field already carries is outside it by construction, not a member it missed — measured above, and the reason the repair is the axis named rather than the claim deleted. The second axis is `seal/ledger/1788700685-two-value-shaped-odd-rows-end-the-report.md`, whose R3 carries the decomposition. **This row narrows to the type axis rather than being re-asserted over the class with the value axis's members ticked off**, and round 1 of that work item is why: the value axis had a third member nobody had measured — a non-finite `usage` field, which ends the report from a `round()` the enumeration's node set could not see — and a row re-asserted over the class would have absorbed it silently. What a row states is only as wide as the method recorded beside it |

<!-- specs/1788632199-the-repository-ships-no-way-to-run-its-own-suite -->
### 1788632199-the-repository-ships-no-way-to-run-its-own-suite

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Eight rows over a branch that is one runner and five documents. What earns a
row here is a judgment whose repair looks obvious from the outside: a warm
call that checks its environment by asking a tool instead of by looking (R1),
an ignore that "uv already writes" (R2), a floor number repeated instead of
named (R3), a fallback command deleted as redundant or kept without its cost
(R4), a rule restated in its second carrier instead of linked (R5), a
runner moved to fix a concealment problem that never existed (R6), a
None-check beside a comparison that reads as redundant (R7), and a paragraph
of vocabulary that reads like a restatement of the table above it (R8).

R2, R3, R5 and R7 carry what review round 1 found: the ignore and the floor
each reached one path and skipped the others, and the class of segments that
run a test had two members and one fix.

R2 and R8 carry what round 2 found. R2's claim was rewritten a second time,
and the rewrite is the point: the ignore had been owed to a LIST of paths,
the list went short twice, and the second miss was created by round 1's own
fix. It is now owed to every exit of one function. R8 is round 2's other
finding read the other way round — the record's cell was correct and the
document that defines it was missing the generator's vocabulary.

R6 is the correction. Phase 1 justified the runner's location with a fact
labelled [executed] that phase 2 falsified with one `ls -a`, and the false
fact had reached three files and a test docstring first. The row states what
the placement actually rests on, so the next reader has the true reason where
the false one was.

Every hash here was stamped by `evidence-check --reverify --ledger '<this
fragment>'`, the scoped WRITE form, and never by an unscoped run; the
unscoped READ form ran afterwards each time, its phase 3 output in
`phases/phase-3.md` and round 1's fix pass in `rounds/round-1.md`. The
scoped form cannot narrow below a file, so a blanket run would re-stamp
`seal/ledger.md`'s one pre-existing drift on behalf of a session that never
read it. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 · `bin/test` pays for its environment once. A warm call decides by the filesystem alone — an interpreter and a `pytest*` script inside `.venv`, and the version `pyvenv.cfg` records — and never reaches the builder, not even to ask a tool whether the environment is current; a cold call reaches it exactly once; and a build that reports success while leaving no pytest behind is a sentence rather than a call that rebuilds forever | `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#has_pytest@2548b29f`, `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#ensure@2c6edb92`, `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#main@323adce0`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_built_environment_is_reused_and_never_rebuilt@d77ca17a`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_missing_environment_is_built_once@0d903e72`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_build_that_leaves_no_pytest_is_a_sentence@5fb2baeb` | **Executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1 (cold 5.24 s, warm 0.60 s and 0.56 s over one 25-case module) and re-measured 2026-09-06 in phase 3 at `a192d47`: two consecutive warm calls of `./bin/test tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py -q` from the worktree, **1.01 s and 0.66 s** wall for 37 cases, the interpreter line naming the reused `.venv` both times. The reuse case fails the run if `build` is reached at all, the cold case asserts it is reached once, and the half-built case exits with `still not in it` rather than looping | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up this row exists against is making `has_pytest` sure.** Its check is `venv_python(venv).exists() and any(scripts.glob("pytest*"))` — filesystem only, no subprocess — and the obvious repair is to run `python -m pytest --version` and read the answer. That pays an interpreter start on every warm call, which is the cost the whole work item removes; the docstring says so where the repair would be typed. **The claim is not that the first call is cheap** — it is that it is the only one. Phase 1's cold figure was taken where uv's wheel cache and a 3.13 interpreter were already present, so on a genuinely cold machine the first call pays what #133 measured and every call after it still pays 0.6 s |
| R2 · the virtualenv is invisible to git on **every exit of `ensure`**, which is every path that can leave one behind, because `main` reaches a virtualenv through that function and through no other. The runner writes the ignore rather than the tool that built it, and writes it from a `finally` rather than from a list of remembered paths — so it covers the build that succeeded, the build that failed partway, the `.venv` merely adopted, the adopted `.venv` the FLOOR **refuses**, and the directory an earlier run left on a machine where neither builder can now finish. `build` keeps a `finally` of its own for the directory it makes. It does nothing when there is no directory, and leaves an existing ignore alone. **Invisible insofar as the write lands**: the guarantee is owed on every exit and delivered on every exit whose `.venv` this process can write to, and a directory the operator has made read-only is theirs — it stays visible to git, and the runner says which file it could not write and what that costs rather than raising over the refusal it has already printed (#177) | `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#hide_from_git@6182d405`, `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#build@ee6d6dd0`, `.github/scripts/run_tests.py#ensure@2c6edb92`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_the_environment_hides_itself_from_git@70a8f5e5`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_an_existing_ignore_is_left_alone@64998dce`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_failed_build_still_leaves_no_trace_in_git@f8e6601a`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_an_adopted_environment_is_hidden_too@f4dbc041`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_refused_environment_is_hidden_too@a4263cef`, `tests/test_the_suite_has_a_command_that_is_cheap_twice.py#test_a_directory_no_builder_can_finish_is_hidden_too@2d872b61` | **Executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 3, both tools measured rather than inherited from prose: `uv venv --python ">=3.12"` (uv 0.10.4) writes `.venv/.gitignore` containing `*`, and `python3.12 -m venv` (3.12.7) writes **no `.gitignore` at all**. ### 1788661274-the-roll-names-the-next-version-by-guessing

<!-- One work item's rows. No header — `fold_ledger.py` writes the `###` at
the release and moves this file into `seal/ledger.md`.

Six rows over a branch that is one script and two documents. What earns a row
here is a judgment whose repair looks obvious from the outside, and this
change has an unusual number of them because every one of its decisions is a
choice between two things that both look correct in the file: a guard that
reads as a special case (G1), a parser that reads as unhelpfully strict (G2),
two `print` calls on a path that exits 0 either way (G3), a constant used once
at each end rather than a literal written once (G4), a format stated in the
tracker document rather than in the skill beside it (G5), and a `gh` argument
that looks like plumbing and is text a person reads (G6).

Four of the six are about the same failure seen from four sides: a roll
that silently does nothing, with the workflow green. That is the class of the
bug being fixed, one step over, and it is what `plan.md` names to design
against.

Nothing from phase 1 or phase 2 reached the ledger, so G1 through G5 are
written in phase 3 against code those phases wrote. The units and cases were
opened at `4695eae` before each row was written, and the two modules were
re-run at the same commit.

**G6, and G2's third instance, are round 1's — written in the fix pass at
`253ac8c`.** G2 said its class was closed at two members and it had three:
the row enumerated what follows the marker and never what precedes it. It now
states the method behind the enumeration — a title is three segments, and a
version comes out of one of them — rather than a list, because a list is what
was complete last time.

Every hash here was stamped by `evidence-check --reverify --ledger '<this
fragment>'`, the scoped WRITE form, and never by an unscoped run; the unscoped
READ form ran afterwards, its output in `phases/phase-3.md`. The scoped form
cannot narrow below a file, so a blanket run would re-stamp `seal/ledger.md`'s
one pre-existing drift on behalf of a session that never read it — and it
would re-stamp F5, whose anchor this branch moved and whose row is edited by
hand for exactly that reason. -->

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 · the roll fires only where a new version has shipped since the open log opened, and it decides that by **equality** — what the open log's title says it rolled from, against the version in the tree — never by comparing two versions in order and never by asking whether this push was a release | `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#roll_is_due@a1bd8c19`, `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#main@aa71fddc`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_a_push_that_shipped_no_new_version_rolls_nothing@a6a6c945`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_a_new_version_since_the_log_opened_rolls_it@6d9c74d3` | Read 2026-09-06 in phase 3 at `4695eae`, the guard and both cases opened together; **executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1, where both cases were seen red before the condition existed, and re-run 2026-09-06 in phase 3 at the same commit: 28 cases in that module, exit 0 | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up is deleting the guard**, because a release always rolls and the branch reads as a special case. That is the code as it stood before #155, and it is the 0.8.1 incident: a patch release closed a log opened for a version that had not shipped, and opened a second one under the identical title. **The second tidy-up is comparing the two versions in order** — `rolled_from(title) < shipped` reads as the same question and is not: it makes the answer depend on version arithmetic at the one moment `docs/branch-and-release.md` says the next number is not knowable, and it is what turns an old-convention title naming `0.9.0` into a log that is never due once `0.9.0` ships. Equality asks only what the title already states. **The third is asking whether this push is a release.** The workflow fires on every push to the default branch, so that fact is not available here; what is available is whether the open log already names the version in the tree |
| G2 · a title this script cannot read as its own is **due**, never silent, and *its own* is the whole of what `log_title` writes, required from the first character. A title is three segments — what precedes the marker, the marker, and what follows it — and a version may be read out of the third only when the first two are exactly what the writer wrote. `rolled_from` answers `None` otherwise, and `None` is a different fact from a version rather than a softer one: not the whole title, not the empty string, and **not a version lifted out of a title with a different prefix** | `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#rolled_from@4ff7e2f2`, `.github/scripts/roll_flow_measurement_issue.py#TITLE_PREFIX@daf589d5`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_rolled_from_reads_only_the_title_the_roll_itself_writes@365dd11b`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_a_title_the_roll_did_not_write_names_no_version@db48506b`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_a_title_the_roll_cannot_read_is_due_rather_than_silent@6a6080bd`, `tests/test_a_release_rolls_the_flow_measurement_issue.py#test_a_title_written_before_this_change_is_always_due@d3813203` | **Executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1: two mutations survived the first loop and both are killed by name — `rolled_from` answering the whole title where the marker is absent, and answering `""` where the marker is there with nothing after it. **Executed** 2026-09-06 in round 1's fix pass: the third instance, found by round 1 and pinned by `test_a_title_the_roll_did_not_write_names_no_version`, plus two more mutations killed at the same time — the `startswith` guard removed, and `.strip()` removed. Read 2026-09-06 at `253ac8c`, the function and its five cases opened together | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up is making `rolled_from` helpful.** Returning the title it was given, or `""`, reads as the forgiving answer and passes every case that goes through `main`, because a wrong answer that is also unequal to the shipped version still comes out as due. The gap opens at a title that happens to BE a version string: read as the log for that version, it is not due, and the log stalls with the workflow green. That is why the pure function is pinned directly rather than through its caller. **The second tidy-up is reading an unreadable title as not due** — do not touch what you do not understand. The two directions are not symmetric: *not due* stops the log forever and nothing goes red, *due* costs at most one roll that was not owed. **The third tidy-up is the one this row missed for a phase and a round — matching the marker anywhere instead of the prefix from the start.** `partition` reads as the same question and is not: it accepts a title with any prefix at all, so `docs: explain flow measurement — after 0.8.2` answered `0.8.2` and the log for 0.8.2 was never due. The row enumerated the segment after the marker, both instances of it, and never looked at the segment before it — which is why the class had a third member the row did not name. One rule then covers four things — an old-convention title, a title somebody tidied by hand, a title this script never wrote at all, and the migration off the old convention, which retires itself at the first release after this change |

### 1788686494-the-printed-ledger-name-collapses-through-relpath

#### The printed ledger name is the file that was read

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A ledger path rendered for a person drops the root's own segments and folds no `..` | `skills/evidence-check/scripts/evidence_check.py#display_name@091b0e46` | **Executed**, by two batteries over the unit's eighteen cases. | R1 · the one write this runner makes to the working tree is guarded, so a `.venv` the operator has made read-only ends the run in a sentence rather than in a traceback under a refusal that had already printed. The refusals above it keep their wording and their exit codes, and the runner does not try to make the write succeed. Green at `8be39a9` with the six `hide_from_git` cases beside them. **Executed** against the real fixture through a probe deleted after it ran — a `.venv` holding an interpreter and a `pytest` script, `pyvenv.cfg` at `version = 3.11.9`, the directory `chmod 555` — two sentences, exit code `2`, no traceback, and `git status --porcelain` reading `?? .venv/`, which is the directory the second sentence promises the reader will see | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up is deleting the `try`**, which reads as defensive programming around a one-line write. `venv_version` guards its read thirty lines up, so the unguarded write was the odd one out rather than the house style — and the write sits in a `finally` that runs on the two exits whose entire product is a sentence, which is what turns one `OSError` into a traceback printed underneath a refusal the reader has already been given. **The second is catching the error and saying nothing.** That leaves the directory in `git status` with nobody told why, which is the silent half of the very defect `seal/ledger.md`'s R2 exists against — the guarantee would then fail invisibly instead of loudly. **The third is testing the directory for writability before writing**: a check that can pass and then fail, and one more thing to keep true, where the write already answers the question. **A `chmod 555` fixture is not a guarantee**, so the case built on one is skipped as root and on Windows with the reason written into the `skipif` rather than beside it, and a second case makes the WRITE refuse by monkeypatching `Path.write_text` so the wording is pinned on all three platforms CI runs. Asserting only *no traceback* would have passed silently wherever `chmod` does not stop a write; asserting the sentence converts that into a case that cannot pass on a write that succeeded |
| R2 · the reach vocabulary `docs/review-chain-spec.md` §*The fix surface* names is derived from what `call_sites`' `return` statements can HAND BACK — not from the strings that appear inside one — so a value the function GAINS has to reach the document before the suite is green again, and a comparison operand or a keyword argument in the same `return` does not. `handed_back` has one arm per way an expression carries a value outward, and everything else arrives as nothing. **The returns it is asked about are the scope's OWN**: `own_returns` stops at every node that opens another function scope, so a `def` written inside `call_sites` no longer hands the derived set a word `call_sites` never returns. The boundary set is two members and not four — only a function body may hold a `return`, and a class or a lambda is reached through a boundary rather than past one, so a member for either is one no mutation can kill. What the derivation cannot see is recorded in `reach_values`' docstring in both directions: five shapes hand a value back across a statement or a call and read as nothing, six more are one `isinstance` arm away, and TWO shapes are read though the function may never hand them back — a call's positional arguments, and a local that shadows a module constant, which derives the module's value. Restored from kept bytes and verified byte for byte. **Executed** 2026-09-06, six mutations over `reach_values` one at a time, each killed by a named case: the named-constant arm deleted, the literal arm deleted, either type check dropped, the return-only filter dropped, the one-definition guard weakened to a truth test, and the derivation emptied. The first run left one survivor — dropping the string check on a literal — because no fixture put a non-string literal in a `return`; the fixture was added and the mutation is dead. **Executed** 2026-09-06 in round 1's fix pass, after the review found the walk collecting every string in a `Return` rather than what one hands back: the four over-collection fixtures — a comparison operand, an f-string, a keyword argument, a dict key — seen red against the blanket walk they replace (`4 failed, 10 passed`), then all ten arms and type guards of `handed_back` mutated one at a time and every one dead.
