Three deliberate survivors, two of which are the grounds for the two-member set: adding `ast.ClassDef` or `ast.Lambda` back changes nothing on any shape a compilable module can hold. The third is the `child.value is not None` conjunct, a type guard rather than a behaviour guard — `handed_back` answers nothing on a `None` through its fallthrough — and `own_returns`' docstring names it as a survivor where round 2's count did not. Round 3 measured both narrowings: dropping the conjunct leaves the module green at 55 passed, and the one shape on which the two boundary sets differ is a `return` written in a class body, which `ast.parse` accepts and `compile` rejects, so no module this can be handed holds one. Both modules green at `106 passed`, exit `0`; `uvx ruff check` and `uvx ruff format --check` exit `0` on every changed file | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up is deleting one of the two cases as a duplicate of the other**, and neither direction is safe — but the reason this row first gave was false, and round 1's finding 1 is that it was asserted rather than measured. Measured: the sentence `only the first is a unit name` is read by the NAMED case alone, so dropping it from the section reddens that one and leaves this one green; a value ADDED to `call_sites` is invisible to the named case and reddens this one; and a rename or a reword reddens BOTH, because the floor here reads the same three constants by attribute name. The false half claimed the rename for the named case alone, and it stood in the docstring where the deletion would be typed. **The second tidy-up is listing the fourth value beside the case** when one is added, which is the defect this row exists against moved one level up: the list would be the thing going stale, and nothing would be reading the generator at all. **The third is the vacuous pass**: a derivation that stops finding anything — `call_sites` renamed, the `return` shapes changed — leaves the assertion loop with nothing to iterate and the case green. So the derived set is required to CONTAIN the three constants the named case reads before the loop runs, which is a floor with content where a bare count would have been a number anybody could lower. **The fourth is deleting the `source` parameter as test-only machinery.** It is what makes the literal arm reachable at all: today's `call_sites` names a constant in every return and writes no literal into one, so against the real module alone that arm is dead code and the mutation deleting it survives — which is what the first mutation run measured, before the fixture that kills it existed. The same parameter is what executes the recorded limit rather than merely stating it |

| Clause | Code grounds | Verified behavior | Checked | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 · a stamp carrying no zone is read as UTC at `parse_time`, the one place in this file where a string becomes a `datetime`, so a naive stamp beside an aware one no longer ends the report. The funnel closes **eight** sites and not the four `plan.md` counted — six subtractions and two orderings — and the ordering half is what the issue's framing missed: a transcript with two calls dies in `load`'s `calls.sort` **before `analyse` is entered at all**, so a guard written at the reported crash site would have left the commoner shape standing. The assumption is stated in the docstring because it is now load-bearing, and what it costs is an absolute time read out of a harness writing local naive stamps — nothing here prints one, since every number this file produces is a difference between two stamps and a difference is right whenever the two share a zone | `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#parse_time@ffbd9c9f`, `skills/verify/scripts/session_cost.py#load@d7f90cd4`, `tests/test_session_cost.py#test_a_naive_stamp_does_not_end_the_report@bea2c63a` | **Executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 1, the case seen red first; **re-executed** 2026-09-06 in phase 2 against the module as `seal/ledger.md`'s R6 was stamped against it (`6863669`), through a probe deleted after it ran, because the crash SITE is what phase 1 corrected and a correction taken on trust is the thing this row exists to stop. A one-call naive transcript exits 1 with stdout empty and `TypeError: can't subtract offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes`; a two-call one exits 1 with stdout empty and `TypeError: can't **compare** offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes`, which is the sort and not a subtraction; `--json` on the one-call shape exits 1 with stdout empty, the same as the report. Against the module now, all three exit 0 — 621 bytes on the one-call report and 745 on the two-call one | 2026-09-06 | **The tidy-up this row exists against is dropping the naive row instead**, the way `parse_time`'s own first sentence drops an unparseable one — it is the shorter edit, it reuses the branch already there, and it reads as consistency with the rule the docstring states. A transcript whose stamps are ALL naive then loses every row and reports nothing, where today it reports numbers that are internally consistent (`plan.md` Alternatives, row 2). **The second is normalising at the call sites**: eight guards and no funnel, which is how these two arrived. **The third is trusting the issue about where the crash is.** #175, `spec.md` §Scope and `plan.md` §Technical context all name `analyse`'s subtractions, and the measurement above says a two-call transcript never reaches `analyse`. The count was low on both halves too — six subtractions, not four, and two orderings the plan does not mention at all |
| R2 · a share of a span is taken through `share`, which returns `—` rather than dividing when the span is **not positive**, so a transcript whose only paired call begins and ends on one timestamp prints the span line, the token block and the family table instead of losing all three behind a `ZeroDivisionError`, and no percentage is invented for a denominator that does not exist. One line under the span says why, because a dash alone poses a question it does not answer — and it is **one line per shape**, since a span of zero and a negative span are not the same reading. The guard on the percentage is on the span being positive rather than non-zero: a negative span — a harness writing a result before the call it answers — is the same undefined division with a sign on it, and one condition covers both there. The SENTENCE is decided separately, in `report`, which is the only place that can tell the two shapes apart. So the row states the node set, which a later session can re-run and widen, instead of a category nobody can check. Chasing a surviving mutation found the other half of that door: the date was read from anywhere in the path, so a dated DIRECTORY exempted every undated file inside it — `docs/experiments/2026-09-03-run/README.md` was exempt. Read from the basename now. Re-executed: the exempt set in the loaded tree is exactly five files, the two dated records plus the three exact paths, and the loaded set still reports no offender. **Re-read 2026-09-07 in review round 2's fix pass, findings 4 and 5, and the token rule moved.** `(?!\w)` is gone. The comment above the constant had stated the disproven `v1.2.30` grounds while the module said the opposite thirty lines down; both now say the same thing. Re-executed over 18 trailing contexts: the removal widens exactly the five prerelease shapes and changes nothing else — `0.9.0.1`, `2.0.1.5`, `v1.2.30`, `0.9.05` and `x0.9.0` all answer as before — and the loaded set still reports no offender. **The leading lookbehind had no case either**, found by mutating this fix: `2.0.1.5`'s tail is below the running version, so the case guarding it answered `[]` with or without the lookbehind. `1.9.9.9`, whose tail is above it, and `x0.9.0` now pin it. **Re-read 2026-09-07, finding 6.** The other-product route could not be taken on the token the refusal prints: the message reports `match.group(0)` — `v9.9.9` — and the lookup read `match.group(1)`, so an author declaring exactly what was printed got no change. Executed both ways round. Either spelling is now accepted. Back to `any()`, so every entry is considered. Executed: with an exact entry written after `docs/experiments/`, the loop answered `False` where `any()` answers `True`. The reviewer's second example, a narrower prefix appended after a wider one, is the same mechanism — an entry below the prefix whose basename carries no date — so one assertion pins both. No instance existed in the tree; the three exact entries sit above the one prefix. **Re-read 2026-09-07, finding 9.** `DATED_RECORD`'s shape was unpinned: dropping its trailing `-` and loosening its component widths each left every case green, and the first silently widened the exemption — `docs/experiments/2026-09-03.md` would have become exempt where it is scanned. The pattern itself is right, being the name `docs/experiments/README.md` prescribes (`<date>-<what-was-asked>.md`), so this was case strength rather than a wrong pattern. Two assertions pin it, and all three mutations of the pattern now turn the case red. No arguments: `"templates/new\nline.md"` and `"templates/\355\225\234\352\270\200.md"`, both quoted and octal-escaped. `-z` alone: every name raw, NUL-separated. `core.quotePath=false` alone: the Korean name raw, and the newline name STILL `"templates/new\nline.md"`. What `-z` does that the config does not is two things: it turns off the escaping of CONTROL characters as well, and it separates on NUL, the one byte a filename cannot hold. The split below the call is on NUL, so dropping `-z` returns the whole listing as a single entry rather than as one entry per file. **This is comment-and-record only and the cases pass either way**, which is exactly how the false sentence sat unnoticed through a round record, a ledger row and a comment. The narrowing removes 56 of 146 occurrences and loses no true positive, which is `plan.md`'s own repair for the false positive it predicts — *narrow the pattern, do not widen the exemption* — taken up front. What it gives up is a one-word unit renamed away, and that is the cheaper mistake: the alternative asks an author to write `NAME NOT IN TREE` beside `rpartition`, which is marker noise on a real name. **The corpus excludes exactly two directories, and they are the two a work item writes about itself** — `seal/specs/` and `seal/ledger/`. `seal/ledger.md` is IN: its S15 note keeps a renamed unit's old name beside the new one on purpose, so a reader coming from an older record can follow it, and excluding the whole of `seal/` refuses five occurrences of that name in work item `1788735085`'s records, every one of them that work item narrating its own rename. The FRAGMENTS are out, and that was found by writing this work item's own rows: doing so put four names into the corpus and silenced four refusals in its own `phase-2.md`, so a work item could clear the check on its records by naming the unit in its own ledger file. The fold moves a fragment into `seal/ledger.md` at the release, which is the same moment the work item stops being live, so nothing changes hands at the boundary. **The 129 occurrences in shipped work items are history and were spot-checked, not assumed.** Six were opened at their coordinates: `header_of`, `row_baseline`, `_correction_traces` and `test_ledger_stamps_resolve` are units that existed when the record was written and have been renamed or removed since; `cmp` and `rpartition` are the false-positive class the narrowing removes |
